> [!embed-item]- Ioun Stone
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Ioun Stone

</div>



# Ioun Stone
*Wondrous item (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/ioun-stone.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/ioun-stone.webp)


Roughly marble sized, *Ioun Stones* are named after Ioun, a god of knowledge and prophecy revered on some worlds. Many types of *Ioun Stones* exist, each type a distinct combination of shape and color.

When you take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to toss an *Ioun Stone* into the air, the stone orbits your head at a distance of `1d3` feet, conferring its benefit to you while doing so. You can have up to three *Ioun Stones* orbiting your head at the same time.

Each *Ioun Stone* orbiting your head is considered to be an object you are wearing. The orbiting stone avoids contact with other creatures and objects, adjusting its orbit to avoid collisions and thwarting all attempts by other creatures to attack or snatch it.

As a [[Rules/Source/actions#Utilize\|Utilize]] action, you can seize and stow any number of *Ioun Stones* orbiting your head. If your [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/attunement-xphb\|Attunement]] to an Ioun Stone ends while it's orbiting your head, the stone falls as though you had dropped it.

The type of stone determines its rarity and effects.

**Items in this group:**

- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-absorption-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Absorption]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-agility-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Agility]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-awareness-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Awareness]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-fortitude-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Fortitude]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-greater-absorption-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Greater Absorption]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-insight-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Insight]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-intellect-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Intellect]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-leadership-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Leadership]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-mastery-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Mastery]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-protection-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Protection]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-regeneration-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Regeneration]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-reserve-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Reserve]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-strength-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Strength]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ioun-stone-sustenance-xdmg\|Ioun Stone, Sustenance]]

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 273. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Iron Bands of Bilarro
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Iron Bands of Bilarro

</div>



# Iron Bands of Bilarro
*Wondrous item, rare*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/iron-bands-of-bilarro.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/iron-bands-of-bilarro.webp)

- **Weight**: 1.0 lbs.

This rusty iron sphere measures 3 inches in diameter and weighs 1 pound. You can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to throw the sphere at a Huge or smaller creature you can see within 60 feet of yourself. As the sphere moves through the air, it opens into a tangle of metal bands.

Make a ranged attack roll with an attack bonus equal to your Dexterity modifier plus your [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/proficiency-xphb\|Proficiency Bonus]]. On a hit, the target has the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Restrained\|Restrained]] condition until you take a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bonus-action-xphb\|Bonus Action]] to issue a command that releases it. Doing so or missing with the attack causes the bands to contract and become a sphere once more.

A creature that can touch the bands, including the one [[Rules/Source/conditions#Restrained\|Restrained]], can take an action to make a DC 20 Strength ([[Rules/Source/skills#Athletics\|Athletics]]) check to break the iron bands. On a successful check, the item is destroyed, and the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Restrained\|Restrained]] creature is freed. On a failed check, any further attempts made by that creature automatically fail until 24 hours have elapsed.

Once the bands are used, they can't be used again until the next dawn.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 274. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> (as) and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Iron Flask
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Iron Flask

</div>



# Iron Flask
*Wondrous item, legendary*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/iron-flask.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/iron-flask.webp)


While holding this brass-stoppered iron flask, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to target a creature that you can see within 60 feet of yourself. If the flask is empty and the target is native to a plane of existence other than the one you're on, the target must succeed on a DC 17 Wisdom saving throw or be trapped in the flask. If the target has been trapped by the flask before, it has [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/advantage-xphb\|Advantage]] on the save. Once trapped, a creature remains in the flask until released. The flask can hold only one creature at a time. A creature trapped in the flask doesn't age and doesn't need to breathe, eat, or drink.

You can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to remove the flask's stopper and release the creature in the flask. The creature then obeys your commands for 1 hour, understanding those commands even if it doesn't know the language in which the commands are given. If you issue no commands or give the creature a command that is likely to result in its death or imprisonment, it defends itself but otherwise takes no actions. At the end of the duration, the creature acts in accordance with its normal disposition and alignment.

An [[Rules/Source/spells/identify-xphb\|Identify]] spell reveals if the flask contains a creature, but the only way to determine the type of creature is to open the flask. A newly discovered Iron Flask might already contain a creature chosen by the DM or determined randomly by rolling on the following table (see the "Monster Manual" for the creature's stat block).

| dice: 1d100 | Contents |
|-------------|----------|
| 01-50 | No creature |
| 51 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/arcanaloth-xmm\|Arcanaloth]] |
| 52-54 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/bone-devil-xmm\|Bone Devil]] |
| 55-56 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/cambion-xmm\|Cambion]] |
| 57-58 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/elemental/dao-xmm\|Dao]] |
| 59 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/celestial/deva-xmm\|Deva]] |
| 60-61 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/elemental/djinni-xmm\|Djinni]] |
| 62-63 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/elemental/efreeti-xmm\|Efreeti]] |
| 64-65 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/erinyes-xmm\|Erinyes]] |
| 66-67 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/giant/fomorian-xmm\|Fomorian]] |
| 68 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/aberration/githyanki-knight-xmm\|Githyanki Knight]] |
| 69 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/aberration/githzerai-zerth-xmm\|Githzerai Zerth]] |
| 70-71 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/glabrezu-xmm\|Glabrezu]] |
| 72-74 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/hezrou-xmm\|Hezrou]] |
| 75 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/incubus-xmm\|Incubus]] |
| 76-77 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/elemental/invisible-stalker-xmm\|Invisible Stalker]] |
| 78-79 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/elemental/marid-xmm\|Marid]] |
| 80 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/marilith-xmm\|Marilith]] |
| 81-82 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/mezzoloth-xmm\|Mezzoloth]] |
| 83-84 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/nalfeshnee-xmm\|Nalfeshnee]] |
| 85-86 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/night-hag-xmm\|Night Hag]] |
| 87-88 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/nycaloth-xmm\|Nycaloth]] |
| 89 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/celestial/planetar-xmm\|Planetar]] |
| 90-91 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/aberration/red-slaad-xmm\|Red Slaad]] |
| 92-93 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/elemental/salamander-xmm\|Salamander]] |
| 94 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/celestial/solar-xmm\|Solar]] |
| 95 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/succubus-xmm\|Succubus]] |
| 96 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/ultroloth-xmm\|Ultroloth]] |
| 97-99 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/fiend/vrock-xmm\|Vrock]] |
| 00 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/elemental/xorn-xmm\|Xorn]] |

> [!embed-item]- Javelin of Lightning

# Javelin of Lightning
*Weapon ([[Rules/Source/items/javelin-xphb\|javelin]]), uncommon*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/javelin-of-lightning.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/javelin-of-lightning.webp)

- **Damage**: 1d6 piercing
- **Range**: 30/120
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Thrown\|Thrown]]
- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.

Each time you make an attack roll with this magic weapon and hit, you can have it deal Lightning damage instead of Piercing damage.

## Lightning Bolt

When you throw this weapon at a target no farther than 120 feet from you, you can forgo making a ranged attack roll and instead turn the weapon into a bolt of lightning. This bolt forms a 5-foot-wide [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/line-area-of-effect-xphb\|Line]] between you and the target. The target and each other creature in the [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/line-area-of-effect-xphb\|Line]] (excluding you) makes a DC 13 Dexterity saving throw, taking `4d6` Lightning damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one. Immediately after dealing this damage, the weapon reappears in your hand. This property can't be used again until the next dawn.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 275. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

This glass jar, 3 inches in diameter, contains `1d4 + 1` doses of a thick mixture that smells faintly of aloe. The jar and its contents weigh 1/2 pound.

As a [[Rules/Source/actions#Utilize\|Utilize]] action, you can swallow one dose of the ointment or apply it to a creature within 5 feet of yourself. The creature that receives it regains `2d8 + 2` [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/hit-points-xphb\|Hit Points]] and ceases to have the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Poisoned\|Poisoned]] condition.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 275*

This lock appears to be an ordinary [[Rules/Source/items/lock-xphb\|Lock]] (of the type described in chapter 6 of the "Player's Handbook") and comes with a single key. # Luck Blade
*Legendary (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/luck-blade.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/luck-blade.webp)


You gain a +1 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. While the weapon is on your person, you also gain a +1 bonus to saving throws.

## Luck

If the weapon is on your person, you can call on its luck (no action required) to reroll one failed [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/d20-test-xphb\|D20 Test]] if you don't have the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Incapacitated\|Incapacitated]] condition. You must use the second roll. Once used, this property can't be used again until the next dawn.

## Wish

The weapon has `1d3` charges. While holding it, you can expend 1 charge and cast [[Rules/Source/spells/wish-xphb\|Wish]] from it. Once used, this property can't be used again until the next dawn. The weapon loses this property if it has no charges.

**Variants**:
- [Luck Glaive](#Luck%20Glaive)
- [Luck Greatsword](#Luck%20Greatsword)
- [Luck Longsword](#Luck%20Longsword)
- [Luck Rapier](#Luck%20Rapier)
- [Luck Scimitar](#Luck%20Scimitar)
- [Luck Shortsword](#Luck%20Shortsword)
- [Luck Sickle](#Luck%20Sickle)

### Luck Glaive

- **Damage**: 1d10 slashing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Heavy\|Heavy]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Reach\|Reach]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Two-Handed\|Two-Handed]]
- **Weight**: 6.0 lbs.

### Luck Greatsword

- **Damage**: 2d6 slashing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Heavy\|Heavy]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Two-Handed\|Two-Handed]]
- **Weight**: 6.0 lbs.

### Luck Longsword

- **Damage**:
  - One-handed: 1d8 slashing
  - Two-handed: 1d10 slashing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Versatile\|Versatile]]
- **Weight**: 3.0 lbs.

### Luck Rapier

- **Damage**: 1d8 piercing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Finesse\|Finesse]]
- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.

### Luck Scimitar

- **Damage**: 1d6 slashing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Finesse\|Finesse]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Light\|Light]]
- **Weight**: 3.0 lbs.

### Luck Shortsword

- **Damage**: 1d6 piercing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Finesse\|Finesse]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Light\|Light]]
- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.

### Luck Sickle

- **Damage**: 1d4 slashing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Light\|Light]]
- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.


*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 275. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

This magic weapon has 3 charges and regains `1d3` expended charges daily at dawn. At the end of each of its turns, a creature repeats the save, ending the effect on itself on a success.

# Manual of Bodily Health
*Wondrous item, very rare*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/manual-of-bodily-health.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/manual-of-bodily-health.webp)

- **Weight**: 5.0 lbs.

This book contains health and diet tips, and its words are charged with magic. If you spend 48 hours over a period of 6 days or fewer studying the book's contents and practicing its guidelines, your Constitution increases by 2, to a maximum of 30. The manual then loses its magic, but regains it in a century.

# Manual of Gainful Exercise
*Wondrous item, very rare*  


- **Weight**: 5.0 lbs.

This book describes fitness exercises, and its words are charged with magic. If you spend 48 hours over a period of 6 days or fewer studying the book's contents and practicing its guidelines, your Strength increases by 2, to a maximum of 30. The manual then loses its magic, but regains it in a century.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 277. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

# Manual of Golems

</div>



# Manual of Golems
*Wondrous item, very rare*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/manual-of-golems.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/manual-of-golems.webp)

- **Weight**: 5.0 lbs.

This tome contains information and incantations necessary to make a particular type of golem. To decipher and use the manual, you must be a spellcaster with at least two 5th-level spell slots. A creature that can't use a manual of golems and attempts to read it takes `6d6` psychic damage.

| dice: d20 | Golem | Time | Cost |
|-----------|-------|------|------|
| 1-5 | [[Rules/Source/items/manual-of-clay-golems-xdmg\|Manual of Clay Golems]] | 30 days | 65,000 GP |
| 6-17 | [[Rules/Source/items/manual-of-flesh-golems-xdmg\|Manual of Flesh Golems]] | 60 days | 50,000 GP |
| 18 | [[Rules/Source/items/manual-of-iron-golems-xdmg\|Manual of Iron Golems]] | 120 days | 100,000 GP |
| 19-20 | [[Rules/Source/items/manual-of-stone-golems-xdmg\|Manual of Stone Golems]] | 90 days | 80,000 GP |

To create a golem, you must spend the time shown on the table, working without interruption with the manual at hand and resting no more than 8 hours per day. You must also pay the specified cost to purchase supplies.

Once you finish creating the golem, the book is consumed in eldritch flames. The golem becomes animate when the ashes of the manual are sprinkled on it. It is under your control, and it understands and obeys your spoken commands.

**Items in this group:**

- [[Rules/Source/items/manual-of-clay-golems-xdmg\|Manual of Clay Golems]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/manual-of-flesh-golems-xdmg\|Manual of Flesh Golems]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/manual-of-iron-golems-xdmg\|Manual of Iron Golems]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/manual-of-stone-golems-xdmg\|Manual of Stone Golems]]

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 277. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

> [!embed-item]- Mirror of Life Trapping
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Mirror of Life Trapping

</div>



# Mirror of Life Trapping
*Wondrous item, very rare*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/mirror-of-life-trapping.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/mirror-of-life-trapping.webp)

- **Weight**: 50.0 lbs.

When this 4-foot-tall, 2-foot-wide mirror is viewed indirectly, its surface shows faint images of creatures. The mirror weighs 50 pounds, and it has AC 11, HP 10, [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/immunity-xphb\|Immunity]] to Poison and Psychic damage, and [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/vulnerability-xphb\|Vulnerability]] to Bludgeoning damage. It shatters and is destroyed when reduced to 0 [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/hit-points-xphb\|Hit Points]].

If the mirror is hanging on a vertical surface and you are within 5 feet of it, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action and use a command word to activate it. It remains activated until you take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action and repeat the command word to deactivate it.

Any creature other than you that sees its reflection in the activated mirror while within 30 feet of the mirror must succeed on a DC 15 Charisma saving throw or be trapped, along with anything it is wearing or carrying, in one of the mirror's twelve extradimensional cells. A creature that knows the mirror's nature makes the save with [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/advantage-xphb\|Advantage]], and Constructs succeed on the save automatically.

An extradimensional cell is an infinite expanse filled with thick fog that reduces visibility to 10 feet. Creatures trapped in the mirror's cells don't age, and they don't need to eat, drink, or sleep. A creature trapped within a cell can escape using magic that permits planar travel. Otherwise, the creature is confined to the cell until freed.

If the mirror traps a creature but its twelve extradimensional cells are already occupied, the mirror frees one trapped creature at random to accommodate the new prisoner. A freed creature appears in an unoccupied space within sight of the mirror but facing away from it. If the mirror is shattered, all creatures it contains are freed and appear in unoccupied spaces near it.

While within 5 feet of the mirror, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to name one creature trapped in it or call out a particular cell by number. The creature named or contained in the named cell appears as an image on the mirror's surface. You and the creature can then communicate.

In a similar way, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action and use a second command word to free one creature trapped in the mirror. The freed creature appears, along with its possessions, in the unoccupied space nearest to the mirror and facing away from it.

Placing the mirror inside an extradimensional space created by a [[Rules/Source/items/bag-of-holding-xdmg\|Bag of Holding]], Portable Hole, or similar item instantly destroys both items and opens a gate to the Astral Plane. The gate originates where the one item was placed inside the other. Any creature within 10 feet of the gate and not behind [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cover-xphb\|Total Cover]] is sucked through it to a random location on the Astral Plane. The gate then closes. The gate is one-way only and can't be reopened.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 278. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

> [!embed-item]- Moonblade
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Moonblade

</div>



# Moonblade
*Legendary (requires attunement by a creature of the weapon's choice)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/moonblade.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/moonblade.webp)


Of all the magic items created by elves, one of the most prized and jealously guarded is a Moonblade. In ancient times, nearly all elven noble houses claimed one such weapon. Over the centuries, some of these weapons have faded from the world, their magic lost as family lines have become extinct. Others have vanished with their bearers during great quests. Thus, only a few of these weapons remain.

Every Moonblade longs for a bearer whose disposition and goals are compatible with its own. If you try to attune to a Moonblade that doesn't want you as its bearer, the weapon not only rejects you but also places a curse on you, causing you to make [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/d20-test-xphb\|D20 Tests]] with [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/disadvantage-xphb\|Disadvantage]] for 24 hours or until the curse is ended by a [[Rules/Source/spells/remove-curse-xphb\|Remove Curse]] spell or similar magic. If you're accepted by the weapon and try to attune to it, you become attuned to it instantly, and a new rune appears on it. You remain attuned to the weapon until you die or the weapon is destroyed. A Moonblade functions like a nonmagical weapon of its kind for anyone other than its chosen bearer.

A Moonblade has one rune on it for each bearer it has willingly served (typically `1d6 + 1`). The first rune grants a +1 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon. Each rune beyond the first grants the Moonblade an additional property. The DM chooses each property or determines it randomly by rolling on the Moonblade Properties table.

## Minor Property

In addition to its aforementioned properties, each Moonblade has a minor property determined by rolling on the Magic Item's Minor Property table.

## Sentience

A Moonblade is a sentient weapon with an Intelligence of 12, a Wisdom of 10, and a Charisma of 12. It has hearing and [[Rules/Source/senses#Darkvision\|Darkvision]] out to 120 feet. Its alignment matches that of its creator.

The weapon communicates by transmitting emotions, sending a tingling sensation through the wielder's hand when it wants to communicate something it has sensed. It can communicate through visions or dreams when the wielder is either in a trance or asleep.

## Personality

A Moonblade has a personality similar to that of its creator. Once a Moonblade has decided on an owner, it believes that only that person should wield it, even if the bearer's alignment differs from that of the weapon's or the bearer's goals later clash with the weapon's goals.

**Moonblade Properties**

| dice: 1d100 | Property |
|-------------|----------|
| 01-60 | Increase the weapon's bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls by 1, to a maximum of +3. Reroll if the *Moonblade* already has a +3 bonus. |
| 61-75 | When you hit with an attack roll using the *Moonblade*, you deal an extra `1d6` Force damage. Each time the weapon gains this property after the first, the extra damage increases by `1d6`, to a maximum of `3d6`. Reroll if the *Moonblade* already deals an extra `3d6` Force damage on a hit. |
| 76-80 | The *Moonblade* gains [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Thrown\|Thrown]] with a normal range of 20 feet and a long range of 60 feet. Each time you throw the weapon, it flies back to your hand after the attack. |
| 81-85 | The *Moonblade* scores a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/critical-hit-xphb\|Critical Hit]] on a roll of 19 or 20 on the `d20`. |
| 86-95 | You can take a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bonus-action-xphb\|Bonus Action]] to cause the *Moonblade* to flash brightly. Each other creature that is within 30 feet of you and not behind [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cover-xphb\|Total Cover]] must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or have the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Blinded\|Blinded]] condition for 1 minute. A creature repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. You can't use this property again until you finish a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/short-rest-xphb\|Short]] or [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/long-rest-xphb\|Long Rest]]. |
| 96-99 | The Moonblade has the properties of a [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-spell-storing-xdmg\|Ring of Spell Storing]]. |
| 100 | You can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to conjure a spectral entity that resembles a shadowy elf if you don't already have one serving you. The entity appears in an unoccupied space within 120 feet of you. It uses the [[Rules/Source/bestiary/undead/shadow-xmm\|Shadow]] stat block with these changes: it is a Fey, has a Neutral alignment, and doesn't create new shadows. You control this entity, deciding how it acts and moves. It remains until it drops to 0 [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/hit-points-xphb\|Hit Points]] or you dismiss it as a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action. |

**Variants**:
- [Moonblade Greatsword](#Moonblade%20Greatsword)
- [Moonblade Longsword](#Moonblade%20Longsword)
- [Moonblade Rapier](#Moonblade%20Rapier)
- [Moonblade Scimitar](#Moonblade%20Scimitar)
- [Moonblade Shortsword](#Moonblade%20Shortsword)

### Moonblade Greatsword

- **Damage**: 2d6 slashing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Heavy\|Heavy]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Two-Handed\|Two-Handed]]
- **Weight**: 6.0 lbs.

### Moonblade Longsword

- **Damage**:
  - One-handed: 1d8 slashing
  - Two-handed: 1d10 slashing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Versatile\|Versatile]]
- **Weight**: 3.0 lbs.

### Moonblade Rapier

- **Damage**: 1d8 piercing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Finesse\|Finesse]]
- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.

### Moonblade Scimitar

- **Damage**: 1d6 slashing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Finesse\|Finesse]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Light\|Light]]
- **Weight**: 3.0 lbs.

### Moonblade Shortsword

- **Damage**: 1d6 piercing
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Finesse\|Finesse]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Light\|Light]]
- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.


*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 279*

</div></div>

> [!embed-item]- Necklace of Fireballs

<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Necklace of Fireballs

</div>



# Necklace of Fireballs
*Wondrous item, rare*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/necklace-of-fireballs.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/necklace-of-fireballs.webp)

- **Weight**: 1.0 lbs.

This necklace has `1d6 + 3` beads hanging from it. You can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to detach a bead and throw it up to 60 feet away. When it reaches the end of its trajectory, the bead detonates as a level 3 [[Rules/Source/spells/fireball-xphb\|Fireball]] (save DC 15).

You can hurl multiple beads, or even the whole necklace, at one time. When you do so, increase the damage of the Fireball by `1d6` for each bead after the first (maximum `12d6`).

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 280. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Necklace of Prayer Beads
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Necklace of Prayer Beads

</div>



# Necklace of Prayer Beads
*Wondrous item, rare (requires attunement by a cleric, druid, or paladin)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/necklace-of-prayer-beads.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/necklace-of-prayer-beads.webp)

- **Weight**: 1.0 lbs.

This necklace has `1d4 + 2` magic beads made from aquamarine, black pearl, or topaz. It also has many nonmagical beads made from stones such as amber, bloodstone, citrine, coral, jade, pearl, or quartz. If a magic bead is removed from the necklace, that bead loses its magic.

Six types of magic beads exist. The DM decides the type of each bead on the necklace or determines it randomly by rolling on the table below. A necklace can have more than one bead of the same type. To use one, you must be wearing the necklace. Each bead contains a spell that you can cast from it as a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bonus-action-xphb\|Bonus Action]] (using your spell save DC if a save is necessary). Once a magic bead's spell is cast, that bead can't be used again until the next dawn.

| dice: 1d20 | Bead | Spell |
|------------|------|-------|
| 1-6 | Bead of Blessing | [[Rules/Source/spells/bless-xphb\|Bless]] |
| 7-12 | Bead of Curing | [[Rules/Source/spells/cure-wounds-xphb\|Cure Wounds]] (level 2 version) |
| 13-16 | Bead of Favor | [[Rules/Source/spells/greater-restoration-xphb\|Greater Restoration]] |
| 17-18 | Bead of Smiting | [[Rules/Source/spells/shining-smite-xphb\|Shining Smite]] |
| 19 | Bead of Summons | [[Rules/Source/spells/guardian-of-faith-xphb\|Guardian of Faith]] |
| 20 | Bead of Wind Walking | [[Rules/Source/spells/wind-walk-xphb\|Wind Walk]] |

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 281. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

# Nine Lives Stealer

# Nine Lives Stealer
*Very rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/nine-lives-stealer.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/nine-lives-stealer.webp)


You gain a +2 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon.

## Life Stealing

The weapon has `1d8 + 1` charges. When you attack a creature that has fewer than 100 [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/hit-points-xphb\|Hit Points]] with this weapon and roll a 20 on the `d20` for the attack roll, the creature must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or be slain instantly as the sword tears its life force from its body. Constructs and Undead succeed on the save automatically. The weapon loses 1 charge if the creature is slain. When the weapon has no charges remaining, it loses this property.

> [!embed-item]- Nolzur's Marvelous Pigments
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Nolzur's Marvelous Pigments

</div>



# Nolzur's Marvelous Pigments
*Wondrous item, very rare*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/nolzurs-marvelous-pigments.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/nolzurs-marvelous-pigments.webp)


This fine wooden box contains `1d4` pots of pigment and a brush (weighing 1 pound in total). Using the brush and expending 1 pot of pigment, you can paint any number of three-dimensional objects and terrain features (such as walls, doors, trees, flowers, weapons, webs, and pits), provided these elements are all confined to a 20-foot [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cube-area-of-effect-xphb\|Cube]]. The effort takes 10 minutes (regardless of the number of elements you create), during which time you must remain in the [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cube-area-of-effect-xphb\|Cube]], and requires [[Rules/Source/conditions#Concentration\|Concentration]]. If your [[Rules/Source/conditions#Concentration\|Concentration]] is broken or you leave the [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cube-area-of-effect-xphb\|Cube]] before the work is done, all the painted elements vanish, and the pot of pigment is wasted.

When the work is done, all the painted objects and terrain features become real. Thus, painting a door on a wall creates an actual door, which can be opened to whatever is beyond. Painting a pit creates a real pit, the entire depth of which must lie within the 20-foot [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cube-area-of-effect-xphb\|Cube]].

No object created by a pot of pigment can have a value greater than 25 GP, and the total value of all objects created by a pot of pigment can't exceed 500 GP. If you paint objects of greater value (such as a large pile of gold), they look authentic, but close inspection reveals they're made from paste, cookies, or some other worthless material.

If you paint a form of energy such as fire or lightning, the energy dissipates as soon as you complete the painting, doing no harm.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 281. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> (as) and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


### O

> [!embed-item]- Oathbow
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Oathbow

</div>



# Oathbow
*Very rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/oathbow.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/oathbow.webp)


When you nock an arrow on this bow, it whispers in Elvish, "Swift defeat to my enemies." When you use this weapon to make a ranged attack, you can utter or sign the following command words: "Swift death to you who have wronged me." The target of your attack becomes your sworn enemy until it dies or until dawn 7 days later. You can have only one such sworn enemy at a time. When your sworn enemy dies, you can choose a new one after the next dawn.

When you make a ranged attack roll with this weapon against your sworn enemy, you have [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/advantage-xphb\|Advantage]] on the roll. In addition, your target gains no benefit from [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cover-xphb\|Half Cover]] or [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cover-xphb\|Three-Quarters Cover]], and you suffer no [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/disadvantage-xphb\|Disadvantage]] due to long range. If the attack hits, your sworn enemy takes an extra `3d6` Piercing damage.

While your sworn enemy lives, you have [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/disadvantage-xphb\|Disadvantage]] on attack rolls with all other weapons.

**Variants**:
- [Oath Longbow](#Oath%20Longbow)
- [Oath Shortbow](#Oath%20Shortbow)

### Oath Longbow

- **Damage**: 1d8 piercing
- **Range**: 150/600
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Ammunition\|Ammunition]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Heavy\|Heavy]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Two-Handed\|Two-Handed]]
- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.

### Oath Shortbow

- **Damage**: 1d6 piercing
- **Range**: 80/320
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Ammunition\|Ammunition]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Two-Handed\|Two-Handed]]
- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.


*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 282. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Oil of Etherealness

</div>



# Oil of Etherealness
*Potion, rare*  


- **Weight**: 0.5 lbs.

One vial of this oil can cover one Medium or smaller creature, along with the equipment it's wearing and carrying (one additional vial is required for each size category above Medium). Applying the oil takes 10 minutes. The affected creature then gains the effect of the [[Rules/Source/spells/etherealness-xphb\|Etherealness]] spell for 1 hour.

Beads of this cloudy, gray oil form on the outside of its container and quickly evaporate.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 282. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Oil of Sharpness
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Oil of Sharpness

</div>



# Oil of Sharpness
*Potion, very rare*  


- **Weight**: 0.5 lbs.

One vial of this oil can coat one Melee weapon or twenty pieces of ammunition, but only ammunition and Melee weapons that are nonmagical and deal Slashing or Piercing damage are affected. Applying the oil takes 1 minute, after which the oil magically seeps into whatever it coats, turning the coated weapon into a +3 Weapon or the coated ammunition into +3 Ammunition.

This clear, gelatinous oil sparkles with tiny, ultrathin silver shards.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 282. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Oil of Slipperiness

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Oil of Slipperiness

</div>



# Oil of Slipperiness
*Potion, uncommon*  


- **Weight**: 0.5 lbs.

One vial of this oil can cover one Medium or smaller creature, along with the equipment it's wearing and carrying (one additional vial is required for each size category above Medium). Applying the oil takes 10 minutes. The affected creature then gains the effect of the [[Rules/Source/spells/freedom-of-movement-xphb\|Freedom of Movement]] spell for 8 hours.

Alternatively, the oil can be poured on the ground as a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action, where it covers a 10-foot square, duplicating the effect of the [[Rules/Source/spells/grease-xphb\|Grease]] spell in that area for 8 hours.

This sticky, black unguent is thick and heavy, but it flows quickly when poured.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 283. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

*Wondrous item, common*

For the duration, you have [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/advantage-xphb\|Advantage]] on all Charisma ([[Rules/Source/skills#Deception\|Deception]] and [[Rules/Source/skills#Persuasion\|Persuasion]]) checks made to influence a creature within 5 feet of yourself.

> [!embed-item]- Pipes of Haunting
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Pipes of Haunting

</div>



# Pipes of Haunting
*Wondrous item, uncommon*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/pipes-of-haunting.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/pipes-of-haunting.webp)


These pipes have 3 charges and regain `1d3` expended charges daily at dawn. You can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to play them and expend 1 charge to create an eerie, spellbinding tune. Each creature of your choice within 30 feet of you must succeed on a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw or have the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Frightened\|Frightened]] condition for 1 minute. A creature that fails the save repeats it at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. A creature that succeeds on its save is immune to the effect of these pipes for 24 hours.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 285. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Pipes of the Sewers
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Pipes of the Sewers

</div>



# Pipes of the Sewers
*Wondrous item, uncommon (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/pipes-of-the-sewers.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/pipes-of-the-sewers.webp)


While these pipes are on your person, ordinary [[Rules/Source/bestiary/beast/rat-xmm\|rats]] and [[Rules/Source/bestiary/beast/giant-rat-xmm\|giant rats]] are [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/indifferent-attitude-xphb\|Indifferent]] toward you and won't attack you unless you threaten or harm them.

The pipes have 3 charges and regain `1d3` expended charges daily at dawn. If you play the pipes as a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action, you can take a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bonus-action-xphb\|Bonus Action]] to expend 1 to 3 charges, calling forth one [[Rules/Source/bestiary/beast/swarm-of-rats-xmm\|Swarm of Rats]] with each expended charge if enough rats are within half a mile of you to be called in this fashion (as determined by the DM). If there aren't enough rats to form a swarm, the charge is wasted. Called swarms move toward the music by the shortest available route but aren't under your control otherwise.

Whenever a Swarm of Rats that isn't under another creature's control comes within 30 feet of you while you are playing the pipes, the swarm makes a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw. On a successful save, the swarm behaves as it normally would and can't be swayed by the pipes' music for the next 24 hours. On a failed save, the swarm is swayed by the pipes' music and becomes [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/friendly-attitude-xphb\|Friendly]] to you and your allies for as long as you continue to play the pipes each round as a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action. A [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/friendly-attitude-xphb\|Friendly]] swarm obeys your commands. If you issue no commands to a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/friendly-attitude-xphb\|Friendly]] swarm, it defends itself but otherwise takes no actions. If a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/friendly-attitude-xphb\|Friendly]] swarm starts its turn more than 30 feet away from you, your control over that swarm ends, and the swarm behaves as it normally would and can't be swayed by the pipes' music for the next 24 hours.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 285. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

> [!embed-item]- Portable Hole
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Portable Hole

</div>



# Portable Hole
*Wondrous item, rare*  



This fine black cloth, soft as silk, is folded up to the dimensions of a handkerchief. It unfolds into a circular sheet 6 feet in diameter.

You can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to unfold a Portable Hole and place it on or against a solid surface, whereupon the Portable Hole creates an extradimensional hole 10 feet deep. The cylindrical space within the hole exists on a different plane of existence, so it can't be used to create open passages. Any creature inside an open Portable Hole can exit the hole by climbing out of it.

You can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to close a Portable Hole by taking hold of the edges of the cloth and folding it up. Folding the cloth closes the hole, and any creatures or objects within remain in the extradimensional space. No matter what's in it, the hole weighs next to nothing.

If the hole is folded up, a creature within the hole's extradimensional space can take an action to make a DC 10 Strength ([[Rules/Source/skills#Athletics\|Athletics]]) check. On a successful check, the creature forces its way out and appears within 5 feet of the Portable Hole. A closed Portable Hole holds enough air for 1 hour of breathing, divided by the number of breathing creatures inside.

Placing a Portable Hole inside an extradimensional space created by a [[Rules/Source/items/bag-of-holding-xdmg\|Bag of Holding]], [[Rules/Source/items/hewards-handy-haversack-xdmg\|Heward's Handy Haversack]], or similar item instantly destroys both items and opens a gate to the Astral Plane. The gate originates where the one item was placed inside the other. Any creature within 10 feet of the gate and not behind [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cover-xphb\|Total Cover]] is sucked through it and deposited in a random location on the Astral Plane. The gate then closes. The gate is one-way only and can't be reopened.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 286. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

# Pot of Awakening
*Wondrous item, common*  



If you plant an ordinary shrub in this 10-pound clay pot and let it grow for 30 days, the shrub magically transforms into an [[Rules/Source/bestiary/plant/awakened-shrub-xmm\|Awakened Shrub]] at the end of that time. When the shrub awakens, its roots break the pot, destroying it.

The awakened shrub is [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/friendly-attitude-xphb\|Friendly]] toward you and obeys your commands. Absent commands from you, it does nothing.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 289*

When you drink this potion, you gain the "enlarge" effect of the [[Rules/Source/spells/enlarge-reduce-xphb\|Enlarge/Reduce]] spell for 10 minutes (no [[Rules/Source/conditions#Concentration\|Concentration]] required).

The red in the potion's liquid continuously expands from a tiny bead to color the clear liquid around it and then contracts. Shaking the bottle fails to interrupt this process.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 288. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

# Potion of Longevity
*Potion, very rare*  


- **Weight**: 0.5 lbs.

When you drink this potion, your physical age is reduced by `1d6 + 6` years, to a minimum of 13 years. Each time you subsequently drink a Potion of Longevity, there is 10 percent cumulative chance that you instead age by `1d6 + 6` years.

Suspended in this amber liquid is a tiny heart that, against all reason, is still beating. These ingredients vanish when the potion is opened.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 288. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

*Wondrous item, common*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/prosthetic-limb.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/prosthetic-limb.webp)


This magic item replaces a lost limb—a hand, an arm, a foot, a leg, or a similar body part. While the prosthetic is attached, it functions identically to the part it replaces. You can detach or reattach it as a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action, and it can't be removed against your will while you are alive.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 290*

| 66-90 | [[Rules/Source/items/quaals-feather-token-tree-xdmg\|Tree]] | Uncommon |

- [[Rules/Source/items/quaals-feather-token-fan-xdmg\|Quaal's Feather Token, Fan]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/quaals-feather-token-swan-boat-xdmg\|Quaal's Feather Token, Swan Boat]]

> [!embed-item]- Quarterstaff of the Acrobat
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Quarterstaff of the Acrobat

</div>



# Quarterstaff of the Acrobat
*Staff, weapon ([[Rules/Source/items/quarterstaff-xphb\|quarterstaff]]), very rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/quarterstaff-of-the-acrobat.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/quarterstaff-of-the-acrobat.webp)

- **Damage**:
  - One-handed: 1d6 bludgeoning
  - Two-handed: 1d8 bludgeoning
- **Range**: 30/120
- **Properties**: [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Thrown\|Thrown]], [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Versatile\|Versatile]]
- **Weight**: 4.0 lbs.

You have a +2 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with this magic weapon.

While holding this weapon, you can cause it to emit green [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/dim-light-xphb\|Dim Light]] out to 10 feet, either as a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bonus-action-xphb\|Bonus Action]] or after you roll [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/initiative-xphb\|Initiative]], or you can extinguish the light as a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bonus-action-xphb\|Bonus Action]].

While holding this weapon, you can take a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bonus-action-xphb\|Bonus Action]] to alter its form, turning it into a 6-inch rod (for ease of storage) or a 10-foot pole, or reverting it a Quarterstaff; the weapon will elongate only as far as the surrounding space allows.

In certain forms, the weapon has the following additional properties.

## Acrobatic Assist (Quarterstaff and 10-Foot Pole Forms Only)

While holding this weapon, you have [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/advantage-xphb\|Advantage]] on Dexterity ([[Rules/Source/skills#Acrobatics\|Acrobatics]]) checks.

## Attack Deflection (Quarterstaff Form Only)

When you are hit by an attack while holding the weapon, you can take a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/reaction-xphb\|Reaction]] to twirl the weapon around you, gaining a +5 bonus to your [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/armor-class-xphb\|Armor Class]] against the triggering attack, potentially causing the attack to miss you. You can't use this property again until you finish a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/short-rest-xphb\|Short]] or [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/long-rest-xphb\|Long Rest]].

## Ranged Weapon (Quarterstaff Form Only)

This weapon has [[Rules/Source/item-properties#Thrown\|Thrown]] with a normal range of 30 feet and a long range of 120 feet. Immediately after you make a ranged attack with the weapon, it flies back to your hand.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 291. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Quiver of Ehlonna
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Quiver of Ehlonna

</div>



# Quiver of Ehlonna
*Wondrous item, uncommon*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/quiver-of-ehlonna.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/quiver-of-ehlonna.webp)

- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.

Each of the quiver's three compartments connects to an extradimensional space that allows the quiver to hold numerous items while never weighing more than 2 pounds. The shortest compartment can hold up to 60 [[Rules/Source/items/arrow-xphb\|Arrows]], [[Rules/Source/items/bolt-xphb\|Bolts]], or similar objects. The midsize compartment holds up to 18 [[Rules/Source/items/javelin-xphb\|Javelins]] or similar objects. The longest compartment holds up to 6 long objects, such as bows, [[Rules/Source/items/quarterstaff-xphb\|Quarterstaffs]], or [[Rules/Source/items/spear-xphb\|Spears]].

You can draw any item the quiver contains as if doing so from a regular quiver or scabbard.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 291. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> (as) and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

This ring has 3 charges, and it regains `1d3` expended charges daily at dawn. While wearing the ring, you can expend 1 charge to cast one of the following spells (save DC 13) from it:

- [[Rules/Source/spells/animal-friendship-xphb\|Animal Friendship]]

> [!embed-item]- Ring of Djinni Summoning
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

# Ring of Djinni Summoning

</div>



# Ring of Djinni Summoning
*Ring, legendary (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-djinni-summoning.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-djinni-summoning.webp)


While wearing this ring, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to summon a particular [[Rules/Source/bestiary/elemental/djinni-xmm\|Djinni]] from the Elemental Plane of Air. The djinni appears in an unoccupied space you choose within 120 feet of yourself. It remains as long as you maintain [[Rules/Source/conditions#Concentration\|Concentration]], to a maximum of 1 hour, or until it drops to 0 [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/hit-points-xphb\|Hit Points]].

While summoned, the djinni is [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/friendly-attitude-xphb\|Friendly]] to you and your allies, and it obeys your commands. If you fail to command it, the djinni defends itself against attackers but takes no other actions.

After the djinni departs, it can't be summoned again for 24 hours, and the ring becomes nonmagical if the djinni dies.

Rings of Djinni Summoning are often created by the djinn they summon and given to mortals as gifts of friendship or tokens of esteem.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 292. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

# Ring of Mind Shielding
*Ring, uncommon (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-mind-shielding.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-mind-shielding.webp)


While wearing this ring, you are immune to magic that allows other creatures to read your thoughts, determine whether you are lying, know your alignment, or know your creature type. Creatures can telepathically communicate with you only if you allow it.

You can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to cause the ring to become imperceptible until you take another [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to make it perceptible, until you remove the ring, or until you die.

If you die while wearing the ring, your soul enters it, unless it already houses a soul. You can remain in the ring or depart for the afterlife. As long as your soul is in the ring, you can telepathically communicate with any creature wearing it. A wearer can't prevent this telepathic communication.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 293. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

# Ring of Regeneration
*Ring, very rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-regeneration.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-regeneration.webp)


While wearing this ring, you regain `1d6` [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/hit-points-xphb\|Hit Points]] every 10 minutes if you have at least 1 [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/hit-points-xphb\|Hit Point]]. If you lose a body part, the ring causes the missing part to regrow and return to full functionality after `1d6 + 1` days if you have at least 1 [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/hit-points-xphb\|Hit Point]] the whole time.

# Ring of Resistance
*Ring, rare*  



You have [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/resistance-xphb\|Resistance]] to one damage type while wearing this ring. The gemstone in the ring indicates the type, which the DM chooses or determines randomly by rolling on the following table.

| dice: d10 | Damage Type | Gem |
|-----------|-------------|-----|
| 1 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-acid-resistance-xdmg\|Acid]] | Pearl |
| 2 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-cold-resistance-xdmg\|Cold]] | Tourmaline |
| 3 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-fire-resistance-xdmg\|Fire]] | Garnet |
| 4 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-force-resistance-xdmg\|Force]] | Sapphire |
| 5 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-lightning-resistance-xdmg\|Lightning]] | Citrine |
| 6 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-necrotic-resistance-xdmg\|Necrotic]] | Jet |
| 7 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-poison-resistance-xdmg\|Poison]] | Amethyst |
| 8 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-psychic-resistance-xdmg\|Psychic]] | Jade |
| 9 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-radiant-resistance-xdmg\|Radiant]] | Topaz |
| 10 | [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-thunder-resistance-xdmg\|Thunder]] | Spinel |
**Items in this group:**

- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-acid-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Acid Resistance]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-cold-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Cold Resistance]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-fire-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Fire Resistance]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-force-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Force Resistance]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-lightning-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Lightning Resistance]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-necrotic-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Necrotic Resistance]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-poison-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Poison Resistance]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-psychic-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Psychic Resistance]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-radiant-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Radiant Resistance]]
- [[Rules/Source/items/ring-of-thunder-resistance-xdmg\|Ring of Thunder Resistance]]

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 294. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

> [!embed-item]- Ring of Shooting Stars
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Ring of Shooting Stars

</div>



# Ring of Shooting Stars
*Ring, very rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-shooting-stars.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-shooting-stars.webp)


You can cast [[Rules/Source/spells/dancing-lights-xphb\|Dancing Lights]] or [[Rules/Source/spells/light-xphb\|Light]] from the ring.

The ring has 6 charges and regains `1d6` expended charges daily at dawn. You can expend its charges to use the properties below.

## Faerie Fire

You can expend 1 charge to cast [[Rules/Source/spells/faerie-fire-xphb\|Faerie Fire]] from the ring.

## Lightning Spheres

You can expend 2 charges as a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to create up to four 3-foot-diameter spheres of lightning.

Each sphere appears in an unoccupied space you can see within 120 feet of yourself. The spheres last as long as you maintain [[Rules/Source/conditions#Concentration\|Concentration]], up to 1 minute. Each sphere sheds [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/dim-light-xphb\|Dim Light]] in a 30-foot radius.

As a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bonus-action-xphb\|Bonus Action]], you can move each sphere up to 30 feet, but no farther than 120 feet away from yourself. The first time the sphere comes within 5 feet of a creature other than you that isn't behind [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cover-xphb\|Total Cover]], the sphere discharges lightning at that creature and disappears. That creature makes a DC 15 Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes Lightning damage based on the number of spheres you created, as shown in the following table. On a successful save, the creature takes half as much damage.

| Number of Spheres | Lightning Damage |
|-------------------|------------------|
| 1 | `4d12` |
| 2 | `5d4` |
| 3 | `2d6` |
| 4 | `2d4` |

## Shooting Stars

You can expend 1 to 3 charges as a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action. For every charge you expend, you launch a glowing mote of light from the ring at a point you can see within 60 feet of yourself. Each creature in a 15-foot [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cube-area-of-effect-xphb\|Cube]] originating from that point is showered in sparks and makes a DC 15 Dexterity saving throw, taking `5d4` Radiant damage on a failed save or half as much damage on a successful one.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 294. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Ring of Spell Storing
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Ring of Spell Storing

</div>



# Ring of Spell Storing
*Ring, rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-spell-storing.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-spell-storing.webp)


This ring stores spells cast into it, holding them until the attuned wearer uses them. The ring can store up to 5 levels worth of spells at a time. When found, it contains `1d6 - 1` levels of stored spells chosen by the DM.

Any creature can cast a spell of level 1 through 5 into the ring by touching the ring as the spell is cast. The spell has no effect other than to be stored in the ring. If the ring can't hold the spell, the spell is expended without effect. The level of the slot used to cast the spell determines how much space it uses.

While wearing this ring, you can cast any spell stored in it. The spell uses the slot level, spell save DC, spell attack bonus, and spellcasting ability of the original caster but is otherwise treated as if you cast the spell. The spell cast from the ring is no longer stored in it, freeing up space.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 295. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

# Ring of Spell Turning
*Ring, legendary (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-spell-turning.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-spell-turning.webp)


While wearing this ring, you have [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/advantage-xphb\|Advantage]] on saving throws against spells. If you succeed on the save for a spell of level 7 or lower, the spell has no effect on you. If that spell targeted only you and didn't create an area of effect, you can take a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/reaction-xphb\|Reaction]] to deflect the spell back at the spell's caster; the caster must make a saving throw against the spell using their own spell save DC.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 295. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

# Ring of the Ram
*Ring, rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-the-ram.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-the-ram.webp)


This ring has 3 charges and regains `1d3` expended charges daily at dawn. While wearing the ring, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to expend 1 to 3 charges to make a ranged spell attack against one creature you can see within 60 feet of yourself. The ring produces a spectral ram's head and makes its attack roll with a +7 bonus. On a hit, for each charge you spend, the target takes `2d10` Force damage and is pushed 5 feet away from you.

Alternatively, you can expend 1 to 3 of the ring's charges as a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to try to break a nonmagical object you can see within 60 feet of yourself that isn't being worn or carried. The ring makes a Strength check with a +5 bonus for each charge you spend.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 296. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

# Ring of X-ray Vision
*Ring, rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-x-ray-vision.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/ring-of-x-ray-vision.webp)


While wearing this ring, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to gain X-ray vision with a range of 30 feet for 1 minute. To you, solid objects within that radius appear transparent and don't prevent light from passing through them. The vision can penetrate 1 foot of stone, 1 inch of common metal, or up to 3 feet of wood or dirt. Thicker substances or a thin sheet of lead block the vision.

Whenever you use the ring again before taking a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/long-rest-xphb\|Long Rest]], you must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or gain 1 [[Rules/Source/conditions#Exhaustion\|Exhaustion]] level.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 296. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

> [!embed-item]- Rival Coin

<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

# Rival Coin

# Rival Coin
*Wondrous item, common*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/rival-coin.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/rival-coin.webp)


This gold coin has a creature embossed on each side. The two depicted creatures must be famous rivals or enemies of each other. For example, a Rival Coin might show "Iggwilv" on one side and "Mordenkainen" on the other, or "Venger" on one side and "Tiamat" on the other. One of these figures is on the "heads" side of the coin, the other on the "tails" side.

The coin has 1 charge and regains its expended charge daily at dawn. You can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to toss the coin, expending its charge. Roll any die to determine whether the coin comes up heads (on an even number) or tails (on an odd number). The roll also determines the effect:

- **Heads.** Target one creature you can see within 60 feet of yourself. The target makes a DC 13 Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, the target takes `2d4` Psychic damage and has [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/disadvantage-xphb\|Disadvantage]] on the next attack roll it makes before the end of its next turn. On a successful save, the target takes half as much damage only.  
- **Tails.** You take `1d4` Psychic damage.  

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 296*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Robe of Eyes

# Robe of Eyes
*Wondrous item, rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/robe-of-eyes.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/robe-of-eyes.webp)


This robe is adorned with eyelike patterns. While you wear the robe, you gain the following benefits:

- **All-Around Vision.** The robe gives you [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/advantage-xphb\|Advantage]] on Wisdom ([[Rules/Source/skills#Perception\|Perception]]) checks that rely on sight.  
- **Special Senses.** You have [[Rules/Source/senses#Darkvision\|Darkvision]] and [[Rules/Source/senses#Truesight\|Truesight]], both with a range of 120 feet.  

## Drawbacks

A [[Rules/Source/spells/light-xphb\|Light]] spell cast on the robe or a [[Rules/Source/spells/daylight-xphb\|Daylight]] spell cast within 5 feet of the robe gives you the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Blinded\|Blinded]] condition for 1 minute. At the end of each of your turns, you make a Constitution saving throw (DC 11 for Light or DC 15 for Daylight), ending the condition on yourself on a success.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 297. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

# Robe of Scintillating Colors
*Wondrous item, very rare (requires attunement)*  



This robe has 3 charges, and it regains `1d3` expended charges daily at dawn. While you wear it, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action and expend 1 charge to cause the garment to display a shifting pattern of dazzling hues until the end of your next turn. During this time, the robe sheds [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bright-light-xphb\|Bright Light]] in a 30-foot radius and [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/dim-light-xphb\|Dim Light]] for an additional 30 feet, and creatures that can see you have [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/disadvantage-xphb\|Disadvantage]] on attack rolls against you. Any creature in the [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bright-light-xphb\|Bright Light]] that can see you when the robe's power is activated must succeed on a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw or have the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Stunned\|Stunned]] condition until the effect ends.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 297. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

# Robe of Stars
*Wondrous item, very rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/robe-of-stars.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/robe-of-stars.webp)


This black or dark-blue robe is embroidered with small white or silver stars. You gain a +1 bonus to saving throws while you wear it.

Six stars, located on the robe's upper-front portion, are particularly large. While wearing this robe, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to remove one of the stars and expend it to cast the level 5 version of [[Rules/Source/spells/magic-missile-xphb\|Magic Missile]]. Daily at dusk, `1d6` removed stars reappear on the robe.

While you wear the robe, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to enter the Astral Plane along with everything you are wearing and carrying. You remain there until you take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to return to the plane you were on. You reappear in the last space you occupied or, if that space is occupied, the nearest unoccupied space.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 297. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>

# Robe of the Archmagi
*Wondrous item, legendary (requires attunement by a sorcerer, warlock, or wizard)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/robe-of-the-archmagi.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/robe-of-the-archmagi.webp)


This elegant garment is made from exquisite cloth and adorned with runes.

You gain these benefits while wearing the robe.

## Armor

If you aren't wearing armor, your base [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/armor-class-xphb\|Armor Class]] is 15 plus your Dexterity modifier.

## Magic Resistance

You have [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/advantage-xphb\|Advantage]] on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

## War Mage

Your spell save DC and spell attack bonus each increase by 2.

>

# Robe of Useful Items

# Robe of Useful Items
*Wondrous item, uncommon*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/robe-of-useful-items.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/robe-of-useful-items.webp)


This robe has cloth patches of various shapes and colors covering it. While wearing the robe, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to detach one of the patches, causing it to become the object or creature it represents. Once the last patch is removed, the robe becomes an ordinary garment.

The robe has two of each of the following patches:

- [[Rules/Source/items/bullseye-lantern-xphb\|Bullseye Lantern]] (filled and lit)  
- [[Rules/Source/items/dagger-xphb\|Dagger]]  
- [[Rules/Source/items/mirror-xphb\|Mirror]]  
- [[Rules/Source/items/pole-xphb\|Pole]]  
- [[Rules/Source/items/rope-xphb\|Rope]] (coiled)  
- [[Rules/Source/items/sack-xphb\|Sack]]  

In addition, the robe has `4d4` other patches. The DM chooses the patches or determines them randomly by rolling on the following table.

| dice: 1d100 | Patch |
|-------------|-------|
| 01-08 | Bag of 100 GP |
| 09-15 | Silver coffer (1 foot long, 6 inches wide and deep) worth 500 GP |
| 16-22 | Iron door (up to 10 feet wide and 10 feet high, barred on one side of your choice), which you can place in an opening you can reach; it conforms to fit the opening, attaching and hinging itself |
| 23-30 | 10 gems worth 100 GP each |
| 31-44 | Wooden ladder (24 feet long) |
| 45-51 | [[Rules/Source/bestiary/beast/riding-horse-xmm\|Riding Horse]] with a [[Rules/Source/items/riding-saddle-xphb\|Riding Saddle]] |
| 52-59 | Open pit (a 10-foot [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/cube-area-of-effect-xphb\|Cube]]), which you can place on the ground within 10 feet of yourself |
| 60-68 | 4 [[Rules/Source/items/potion-of-healing-xdmg\|Potions of Healing]] |
| 69-75 | [[Rules/Source/items/rowboat-xphb\|Rowboat]] (12 feet long) |
| 76-83 | [[Rules/Source/items/spell-scroll-xdmg\|Spell Scroll]] containing one spell of level 1, 2, or 3 (your choice) |
| 84-90 | 2 [[Rules/Source/bestiary/beast/mastiff-xmm\|Mastiffs]] |
| 91-96 | Window (2 feet by 4 feet, up to 2 feet deep), which you can place on a vertical surface you can reach |
| 97-00 | [[Rules/Source/items/portable-ram-xphb\|Portable Ram]] |

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 298. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Rod of Absorption

# Rod of Absorption
*Rod, very rare (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/rod-of-absorption.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/rod-of-absorption.webp)

- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.

While holding this rod, you can take a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/reaction-xphb\|Reaction]] to absorb a spell that is targeting only you and doesn't create an area of effect. The absorbed spell's effect is canceled, and the spell's energy—not the spell itself—is stored in the rod. The energy has the same level as the spell when it was cast. A canceled spell dissipates with no effect, and any resources used to cast it are wasted. The rod can absorb and store up to 50 levels of energy over the course of its existence. Once the rod absorbs 50 levels of energy, it can't absorb more. If you are targeted by a spell that the rod can't store, the rod has no effect on that spell.

When you become attuned to the rod, you know how many levels of energy the rod has absorbed over the course of its existence and how many levels of spell energy it currently has stored.

If you are a spellcaster holding the rod, you can convert energy stored in it into spell slots to cast spells you have prepared or know. You can create spell slots only of a level equal to or lower than your own spell slots, up to a maximum of level 5. You use the stored levels in place of your slots but otherwise cast the spell as normal. For example, you can use 3 levels stored in the rod as a level 3 spell slot.

A newly found rod typically has `1d10` levels of spell energy stored in it. A rod that can no longer absorb spell energy and has no energy remaining becomes nonmagical.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 299. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Rod of Alertness
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Rod of Alertness

</div>



# Rod of Alertness
*Rod, very rare (requires attunement)*  


- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.

This rod has the following properties.

## Alertness

While holding the rod, you have [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/advantage-xphb\|Advantage]] on Wisdom ([[Rules/Source/skills#Perception\|Perception]]) checks and on [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/initiative-xphb\|Initiative]] rolls.

## Spells

While holding the rod, you can cast the following spells from it:

- [[Rules/Source/spells/detect-evil-and-good-xphb\|Detect Evil and Good]]  
- [[Rules/Source/spells/detect-magic-xphb\|Detect Magic]]  
- [[Rules/Source/spells/detect-poison-and-disease-xphb\|Detect Poison and Disease]]  
- [[Rules/Source/spells/see-invisibility-xphb\|See Invisibility]]  

## Protective Aura

As a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action, you can plant the haft end of the rod in the ground, whereupon the rod's head sheds [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bright-light-xphb\|Bright Light]] in a 60-foot radius and [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/dim-light-xphb\|Dim Light]] for an additional 60 feet. While in that [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bright-light-xphb\|Bright Light]], you and your allies gain a +1 bonus to [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/armor-class-xphb\|Armor Class]] and saving throws and can sense the location of any [[Rules/Source/conditions#Invisible\|Invisible]] creature that is also in the [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bright-light-xphb\|Bright Light]].

The rod's head stops glowing and the effect ends after 10 minutes or when a creature takes a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to pull the rod from the ground. Once used, this property can't be used again until the next dawn.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 299. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>


> [!embed-item]- Rod of Lordly Might
> 
<div class="transclusion internal-embed is-loaded"><div class="markdown-embed">

<div class="markdown-embed-title">

# Rod of Lordly Might

</div>



# Rod of Lordly Might
*Rod, legendary (requires attunement)*  
![/Rules/Source/items/img/rod-of-lordly-might.webp\|right wmed](/img/user/Rules/Source/items/img/rod-of-lordly-might.webp)

- **Weight**: 2.0 lbs.

This rod has a flanged head, and it functions as a magic Mace that grants a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with it. The rod has properties associated with six different buttons that are set in a row along the haft. It has three other properties as well, detailed below.

## Buttons

You can press one of the following buttons as a [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bonus-action-xphb\|Bonus Action]]; a button's effect lasts until you push a different button or until you push the same button again, which causes the rod to revert to its normal form:

- **Button 1.** A fiery blade sprouts from the end opposite the rod's flanged head. The flames shed [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/bright-light-xphb\|Bright Light]] in a 40-foot radius and [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/dim-light-xphb\|Dim Light]] for an additional 40 feet, and the blade functions as a magic Longsword or Shortsword (your choice) that deals an extra `2d6` Fire damage on a hit.  
- **Button 2.** The rod's flanged head folds down and two crescent-shaped blades spring out, transforming the rod into a magic Battleaxe that grants a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with it.  
- **Button 3.** The rod's flanged head folds down, a spear point springs from the rod's tip, and the rod's handle lengthens into a 6-foot haft, transforming the rod into a magic Spear that grants a +3 bonus to attack rolls and damage rolls made with it.  
- **Button 4.** The rod transforms into a climbing pole up to 50 feet long (you specify the length), though the rod's buttons remain within your reach. In surfaces as hard as granite, a spike at the bottom and three hooks at the top anchor the pole. Horizontal bars 3 inches long fold out from the sides, 1 foot apart, forming a ladder. The pole can bear up to 4,000 pounds. More weight or lack of solid anchoring causes the rod to revert to its normal form.  
- **Button 5.** The rod transforms into a handheld battering ram and grants its user a +10 bonus to Strength ([[Rules/Source/skills#Athletics\|Athletics]]) checks made to break through doors, barricades, and other barriers.  
- **Button 6.** The rod assumes or remains in its normal form and indicates magnetic north. (Nothing happens if this function of the rod is used in a location that has no magnetic north.) The rod also gives you knowledge of your approximate depth beneath the ground or your height above it.  

## Drain Life

When you hit a creature with a melee attack using the rod, you can force the target to make a DC 17 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the target takes an extra `4d6` Necrotic damage, and you regain a number of [[Rules/Source/variant-rules/hit-points-xphb\|Hit Points]] equal to half that Necrotic damage. Once used, this property can't be used again until the next dawn.

## Paralyze

When you hit a creature with a melee attack using the rod, you can force the target to make a DC 17 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the target has the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Paralyzed\|Paralyzed]] condition for 1 minute. The target repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success. Once used, this property can't be used again until the next dawn.

## Terrify

While holding the rod, you can take a [[Rules/Source/actions#Magic\|Magic]] action to force each creature you can see within 30 feet of yourself to make a DC 17 Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, a target has the [[Rules/Source/conditions#Frightened\|Frightened]] condition for 1 minute. A [[Rules/Source/conditions#Frightened\|Frightened]] target repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. Once used, this property can't be used again until the next dawn.

*Source: Dungeon Master's Guide (2024) p. 300. Available in the <span title='Systems Reference Document (5.2)'>SRD</span> and the Free Rules (2024)*

</div></div>
